ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u42	You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u43	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The question concerns one of the most important aspects of the functioning of the modern army and the entire national defence department. The training of officers and the training of managers or commanders in the army must be based on three main issues. The first issue is knowledge which must take into account the intellectual level, which must be based on the latest achievements of science and must be linked to the broad context of general knowledge. The second thing is a military craft. This is all that consists of the knowledge or skill of an officer or an officer, including rigor and discipline, specific military skills, everything that makes a candidate for this profession a true commander and leader in the army. And finally, the third thing that is essentially important is upbringing. Officer schools differ from civil schools, public schools, that in addition to the transfer of knowledge and training of specific skills, they must shape a patriotic and civic attitude. Military schools must not give up, unlike civilian schools, from universities, polytechnics and other higher schools, from shaping attitudes and raising these officers. At the moment, the military education system is undergoing rapid changes adapting universities to the requirements of the modern battlefield, the challenges facing the Polish state, and at the same time it needs to take clear account of new changes in the programme, teaching staff and how these schools are organised and financed. At the same time, these changes that are taking place at the moment, because schools do not have the right to stop functioning, to maintain continuity. Where necessary, this continuity is maintained to a large extent. Where there are necessary deeper changes, we make them in a quick and directly related mode for a given school. At the moment we have five officer schools: Military Technical Academy in Warsaw, Academy of War Art in Rembertów, Higher Officer School of Land Forces in Wrocław, Navy Academy in Gdynia and Orląt School in Dęblin training staff for the use of the Air Force. We really want every one of these schools to grow in the number of sick people who end up with officers. In the current year's year, the number of patients was increased by almost 50%, that's forty-two percent more than a year ago. This trend will also be maintained next year. At the same time, at the same time in a reasonable and very well-targeted manner, we reduce the number of civilian students. Civil students at military universities are needed, we don't want to give up on them at all. They bring a different way of looking, freedom of discussion, linking an officer's school with the entire civilian community, so that civilian students are not only welcome in military universities, but even needed. On the other hand, it has been so far that civilian students have dominated, both in terms of the way the school operates and in terms of a certain atmosphere, also when it comes to programs. In earlier officer schools have existed so far, and they still exist, because we cannot extinguish them immediately, various kinds of teachings that do not have anything in common with the army. These directions will be slowly, quietly extinguished. At the same time, I would like to make it clear that the reduction in the number of civilian students will be followed by a reduction in subsidies from the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, and therefore the funding from the Ministry of National Defence must increase. And all of this, including investment changes -- it's about teaching rooms, dorms, cafeterias -- it's a process that's going on right now. We increase efficiency, adapt these schools to new conditions, increase subsidies from the Ministry of Defence and do so in a very consistent and very efficient way. I very much hope that during this academic year the process of modifying the activities and reconstruction of the original officer profile of these schools will be completed. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u80	You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u81	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The problem raised by the Member is a matter of interest to us. For months, we have been exploring ways to improve the work of institutions related to young people who have been in some kind of conflict with the law. What is the process of rehabilitation of minors through work? Juvenile correction facilities and shelters, using existing legal status, enable their pupils to train and acquire their profession, thus increasing the chance of full social integration after leaving the facility. All betting pupils go to them with educational arrears, so more emphasis is placed on their education. In establishments and shelters always work primary schools, middle school, vocational schools. There are also practices that teach, adapt to professions that are wanted on the market. School workshops work within schools, where minors acquire practical skills. In addition, minors can work in voluntary or business associations. Then they may also receive remuneration. Minors placed in hostels to self-emancipate undertake work with an outside employer. Between 2010 and 2014, pupils participated in vocational courses and trainings co-financed by EU funds. Some of them continue today as well. These are trainings, among others. for a paver, hairdresser, conservator, interior contractor, these are courses of cutting and sewing, painting, upholstery, but also courses for the guardian of children, carpenter or locksmith. In answer to the question whether we are planning to organise the legislative sphere in a comprehensive manner - yes, at the moment a draft regulation has been drawn up in the Ministry of Justice, according to which a team will be set up, which will also include people from the Ministry of Education who are subject to the agreement in the educational part. New rules are to be developed for the functioning and cooperation of these institutions, the whole area of functioning of the state, which is responsible for minors who have fallen into some kind of conflict with the law. Our task, of course, will be to achieve a more effective rehabilitation of these people, including through what you have touched, that is, through work. Here we agree with the honourable Member that there should be more emphasis on the search for work, which will be adapted to the aspirations of those who are in the correction facilities. We agree that this is the right way to ensure that this young man finds his place in society and does not fall back into conflict with the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u112	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The system of state registers was launched in the municipalities in March 2015. It has been improved and updated so far. The Central Information Technology Centre is responsible for collecting information from municipal officials on the functioning of the system. An important problem is the lack of the possibility of reversing the mistaken operations carried out in the system, in particular those concerning the annulment of the identity card. Unfortunately, today is not possible. Any one of us could lose his ID someday. However, it should be borne in mind that when we declare it invalid, we are doing the irreparable work recorded in the Register of Personal Evidence. This means that if a document is found, it becomes useless to us. Officials described to me that a mistake could also happen. when issuing a new identity card when the new document is annulled instead of the old document. As a result, the penthouse stays with two invalid blanks. All officials are connected to one central IT system, so it is also possible to mistakenly invalidate the identity card of the resident of another municipality, which in turn poses additional problems. I would therefore like to ask: Is the Ministry planning to take action to resolve this small problem, as we believe? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u113	I'm asking you to answer me.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u114	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. Still important to you, sir. I reply on behalf of the Ministry of Digitalization, but this reply is also agreed here with the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration as part of our approach to this, leaving the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, so that it will be as complete as possible. In the first place, the question arises as to the purpose of allowing the official to restore the validity of the proof, i.e. what scale of this phenomenon we are talking about here. So we do not have such an analysis, but I can say that as part of the team working on it - a team working in the composition of the Central Information Technology Centre, the Ministry of Digitalization and representatives of the offices for which the system of state registers is a tool of work - they are analysed on an ongoing basis. I know that the subject appeared during the meetings of this team, but it was not considered critical or urgent. And please note that as far as the system of state registers is concerned, it is actually being intensively improved in the last year. 900 applications were made this year and among them there was no such topic. In any case, however, this correction would require an in-depth analysis of both legislation, because we already know that changes would be necessary there, but also the safety of legal and technical trade. These changes, the analysis of these changes, seem to make sense when we actually confirm that the scale of this phenomenon has a wider dimension. With regard to what it looks like now, please take into account that at this point in time in the Register of Personal Evidence, such solutions are applied which force the official to have a sequence of actions and to confirm, to sign those actions which he carries out, which to a significant extent causes that it can be avoided as a result of a mistrial of the document. That's one thing. On the other hand, let us also bear in mind that the annulment of the document also has some consequences at once, and the official may realize that he made this mistake only after some time, when this information about the annulment is already distributed further, as the invalid identity card is, among other things. entered in the register of invalid identity cards, which is already made available in real time as a service e.g. for banks, even in the process of applying for credit. Many banks have implemented a verification service for identity card validity, as it is possible to verify this, as I say, in real time. The second consequence is that at the time when the document is cancelled is due to the loss of the document or to the fact that it is declared missing, such a document is transmitted to the Schengen Information System as a wanted document. And now, if we wanted to restore the validity of the evidence, it would also require building a communication process and like canceling an entry in that register, which would also not be a procedure that could be applied immediately in real time. The second thing I would also like to point out is that it is precisely because the statement that the document should be restored, and the fact that it has been annulled, these are two events disconnected over time, that we could allow doubts to arise as to the validity of this document at the time of some activity based on this document. Another thing that is worth pointing out is that this could lead to some kind of abuse if the civil servant's freedom of action was too great here. On the other hand, if the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration were to decide to make appropriate changes to the law that would allow this technical change, the Ministry of Digitalization is here in the view that this activity should not be carried out by a staff member of the municipality's office, but it should be a specific procedure which, in justified cases, would require notification of a wish to restore the validity of the proof to the Ministry of Digitalization and this ministry as administrator, the staff member of this ministry would only be able to make this change. I think that this is the only way to minimise the risk of abuse when making these corrections and coordinate these corrections also in other systems. In conclusion, the possibility of restoring the validity of personal evidence at this point is not planned, and a detailed analysis of the possible change can be undertaken when we confirm that the scale of the phenomenon and the urgency of the solution are actually sufficient and relevant. So much for me. Thank you for your attention.

This is only an improvement and adaptation to the new regulation of the European Union. Will this change the situation of farmers in a profound way? To ensure that farmers have more fluidity in this difficult European situation that has arisen in agriculture. So the words about some great help are a little inadequate here. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u333	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the reports of the Committee on Public Finance on Parliamentary Bills: on the National Tax Administration, print No 925, and the Rules introducing the Act on National Tax Administration, print No 926. The basis for the proposed regulation establishing the National Tax Administration lies directly under Article. 84 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland is the importance of the obligation to bear public benefits, in particular taxes and customs duties. In the current state of law, the tax and customs administration which collects budgetary revenue within the government has an on-the-spot structure, distributed, concentrated in three independently functioning verticals. The first is the tax administration, the second is the customs service and the third is the tax control. The dispersal and duplication of certain tasks related to the collection of tax and customs duties within each service makes it impossible to carry them out in a coherent and uniform manner, while at the same time making optimal use of available staff and organisational and financial capacity. The simultaneous functioning of several identical control procedures towards taxpayers fosters the emergence of significant procedural differences that affect taxpayers' rights and obligations. Maintaining the current state in which the tax, customs and fiscal administrations are de facto not acting according to uniform procedures undoubtedly has a negative impact on the level of collection of tax liabilities due and thus on the state budget. Therefore, a project was created proposing the creation of a consolidated administration in the form of the National Tax Administration. After the first reading at the meeting of the Sejm, the draft law was addressed to the Committee on Public Finance, which set up an extraordinary subcommittee for the examination of parliamentary draft laws: on the National Tax Administration (Print No 826) and the Rules introducing the Act on National Tax Administration (Print No 827). The subcommittee, in order to discuss in detail 276 articles contained in the original draft law, held three full-day meetings with representatives of numerous social parties, mainly trade unions of customs and fiscal services. During the procedure, Members tabled several dozen amendments, which were introduced in the draft law after the votes. The report of the subcommittee, together with the attached draft law and the proposal to adopt the bill, was presented at the meeting of the Public Finance Commission on 18 October this year. During the multi-hour examination by the Committee on Public Finance, the various articles of the draft law were tabled by the members of the committee by successive amendments. Discussion of the draft law by the subcommittee and the amendments tabled also took place with the participation of numerous representatives of the social side. Representatives of the trade unions of customs and fiscal services took an active part in the discussion. The Commission, after considering the draft law at its meetings on 22 September and 18 October 2016, requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. The same procedure concerned the draft law by the introductory provisions. At the same time, similar meetings were held and the Committee on Public Finances also decided at its meeting on 18 October to present the bill to the High House with a recommendation for the adoption of this draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u348	Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u349	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law on National Tax Administration and the draft law on National Tax Administration. Reform of the danin collection and control system is needed and expected. Sealing the revenue system to the budget is a necessity. We all expect a simple and efficient system operating at no excessive cost. Consolidation of the tax administration, tax control and customs service is, in our view, a good solution. Improving the coherence of tax and customs collection tasks, making better use of human resources and technical resources is a good direction. Currently, the tax administration has 16 tax houses and 400 tax offices, the Customs Service is 16 customs chambers, 45 customs offices and 141 customs departments. The tax control is carried out by 16 tax offices and eight branches. All units at voivodship level have their own budgets, staff and manage assets. It's just a waste. The implementation of tax and customs collection tasks is inconsistent, each of these authorities pursues its own development policy and has its own low-compatibility IT solutions for data exchange. The draft law provides for the establishment of the National Tax Administration in place of the current tax administration, tax control and customs services. The National Tax Administration will be subordinate to the Minister responsible for public finances. In accordance with the draft as of 1 January 2017, they will be merged at the level of each voivodeship of the tax administration chamber with customs offices and tax control offices. The number of administrative units will decrease, which is the implementation of requests made for many years by numerous environments as regards the need to reduce administrative burdens. This direction of change should also be maintained in other areas of public administration, including local government. Unfortunately, the project also contains a dangerous solution to the security of the situation of citizens, which is the abolition of the two-instant tax procedure. Appeals against decisions of heads of customs offices, successors of tax offices, i.e. the authority that will conduct the biggest, most difficult proceedings, will be dealt with by the same chief. This will result in different review procedures in the case of tax proceedings. If we have to deal with proceedings conducted by the head of the customs office, the appeal will be dealt with by the same authority. If the proceedings are conducted by the head of the tax office, the appeal against the decision will be sent to the head of the tax administration. The result will be a quick enforcement of tax charges also in the case of incorrect decisions. In this part, the project should be considered and, in our view, revised during further work in the Parliamentary Committee to guarantee the security of taxpayers and the stability of legal trading, which translates into the level of confidence of citizens in the institutions of the state. The Parliamentary draft law Rules introducing the National Tax Administration Act contains the necessary amendments to 157 laws related to the entry into force of the National Tax Administration Act. Among other things, it protects the interests of workers employed in the combined institutions so far. The bill provides for a simplification of the appeal procedure, which will be an effective step towards shortening the proceedings, in line with the expectations of taxpayers who have repeatedly applied for their duration, which is the main concern of the Polish authorities on the way to providing the budget with statutory public tributes. The above-mentioned draft laws are a step in the right direction, towards a deep reform of the tax administration, which has been raised by the environment concerned for many years. Their adoption will increase consistency in the implementation of tax and customs collection tasks and improve the use of staff and technical resources of the combined institutions. However, in our view, the draft laws submitted should be amended in relation to the issues which I have raised regarding ensuring the certainty of the legal circulation and the security of taxpayers as parties to the proceedings. The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle, subject to changes in the draft laws I mentioned, will support the submitted projects. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u361	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have a question about the article. 172 introductory provisions. Under this provision, there is an actual verification of the employees of the fiscal apparatus. While tax and customs officials become employees or officials of the National Tax Administration, the continuation of employment or service relationships is subject to a written proposal from the competent authority for new working or service conditions. If such a proposal is not made or is not accepted by a staff member or officer, the relationship shall cease. It is important that the submission or non-submission of this proposal is left to the discretion of the legal authority in relation to the employment or service relationship and that the absence of a declaration of acceptance or refusal of acceptance of the proposal leads to the termination of the employment relationship. Such solutions should be regarded as unacceptable, for they give rise to unwarranted uncertainty on the part of workers and officers. Don't you think that this impreciseness and great freedom of the authorities presenting a proposal for new working conditions or services undoubtedly undermine the interests of the weaker party in the relationship of work, i.e. the worker? In our view, each person should receive a proposal for new working conditions, especially since the project does not foresee any criteria to be followed in the selection of employees to whom new working conditions will be proposed. Do you not think that it is necessary in such a situation to ensure proper control of the acts entrusting these relations to work by independent courts - labour courts? Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u387	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the applicants, I have the honour to present the justification of the Commission's draft law on amending the Polish Charter Act and the Foreigners Act, contained in print No. 904. Commission for Communication with Poles Abroad at its meeting of 13 September this year. took the legislative initiative to amend the Polish Charter Act of 7 September 2007 - hence the conclusion that September is a good month for the Polish Charter. The amendment is the result of discussions between the Commission and the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration and clarification of the law on the competence of voivodships to issue decisions on monetary benefits for holders of the Polish Charter. In order to justify the need for the proposed amendments, I will quote the most important points in the explanatory memorandum of the bill. The Act on the Charter of a Pole in the current normative form provides that the authority competent to issue decisions granting the benefit to holders of the Charter of a Pole settling in Poland and to pay it shall be the head of the person entitled to the benefit in Poland. With a view to linking the entitlement to the benefit of money to the fact that an application for a permanent residence permit for which the voivode has jurisdiction, it is appropriate that the voivode be also the granting authority and that the oldosta would remain the competent authority for its payment. Secondly, the existing provisions of the Polish Charter Act do not provide for mechanisms to eliminate situations of multiple applications for a monetary benefit by the same person, e.g. to several persons and do not restrict the circle entitled to apply for benefits to persons who apply for permanent residence permits after the entry into force of the provisions of the Act on the possibility of applying for benefits. Leaving unregulated e.g. the issue may lead to a situation in which persons who have applied for and obtained permanent residence permits for the possession of the Polish Charter in previous years will also apply for benefits. That would be over 10,000 in total. persons from 2014 to 2016, which would be contrary to the objective mentioned above. benefits, which are aid during the initial period of stay in Poland to the holders of the Polish Charter, who decided to settle in our country. Amendments proposed in Article 24c are the consequence of the appointment of the voivode in place of the old-fashioned as the authority competent to receive and deal with applications for a cash benefit. It is proposed that the governor informs the voivode of the level of use of the grant for payment of cash benefits and that the voivodship informs the Minister responsible for home affairs of the amount necessary to secure the payment of cash benefits resulting from applications for money benefits and the level of use of the grant by the starosts. Time limits for the presentation of the above mentioned document: the information shall be specified in the implementing rules issued pursuant to Article. 24d Act on the Polish Charter. And one last thing. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 24d proposed an amendment to the delegation for the Minister for Home Affairs to the regulation on cases relating to the granting of grants to cover the payment of cash benefits by clarifying that the above mentioned. The Regulation will also specify the detailed arrangements for the allocation of the special-purpose reserve for the payment of cash benefits and for the transfer of funds to cover expenditure related to the provision of these benefits. Finally, I would like to say that the amendment that we are working on today, in my opinion, is mainly a clarification of the current shape of the Polish Charter Act. I ask the High House to support the amendments presented. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u391	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Civic Platform club in relation to the amended Polish Charter Act, print No. 904. The amendment is necessary because the work on the bill was hasty and therefore many provisions have not been clarified. The government has prepared an amendment and put it into the hands of Members so that this road, this legislative procedure, can be carried out faster. What does he notice? The explanatory memorandum states that three issues need to be settled. It seems that not only formal or minor, but serious, legislative. The first point is to identify the voivode instead of the old-fashioned one as the one appropriate for granting cash benefits. The second point is to provide mechanisms to ensure the efficient use of budgetary resources for the payment of benefits. Finally, the third issue, namely to ensure effective monitoring of the use of the limits of resources planned in the special-purpose reserve for the payment of these benefits. In this arrangement, I'm going to analyze these newization records. The indication of voivodeship instead of old age, in short, will result in a significant extension of the administrative process and will be very burdensome for the beneficiaries. Why? Well, the application which should be submitted to the old man, because it is to be the closest to the place of residence, will be submitted to the voivodeship. For what reason? From the fact that an application for permanent residence will also be submitted to the voivodeship. It is true, but there will be two applications for permanent residence and cash benefits. At the same time, these conclusions will have to be evaluated by someone. By who? By the governor responsible for the beneficiary's place of residence. So the voivode will be sending questions to the old man. The Starosta will collect materials that he will send to the voivodeship. The governor will analyze them and send the money to the old town. And that's half poor, but let's imagine what happens when the beneficiary leaves for another voivodeship and doesn't have a bank account. Then he'll have to come every month to collect the money. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u399	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Mr. Speaker! This project concerns improvements in the functioning of the Polish Charter Act of 7 September 2007. Changes are envisaged for, among others, the designation of the voivode instead of the old-fashioned one as the competent authority for granting the money benefit and the establishment of the Minister responsible for home affairs as a higher authority in these matters. In addition, the procedure for recording the fact that an application for a benefit has been submitted and the decision has been taken in the relevant register or records has been clarified. The introduction of these regulations will eliminate multiple requests for a cash benefit by the same person. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the solutions submitted, but the procedure for the Polish Charter Act also raises questions as to whether the measures applied are appropriate to the policy addressed to the Polish Republic. The existing instruments contained in the Polish Charter appear to serve citizens of neighbouring countries more to obtain certain privileges, in particular workers, than to create a global community of people who identify with the Polish national community. This calls for reflection and reflection on the policy of the Polish state in terms of communication with Poland and Poles abroad. Moreover, obtaining the rights deriving from the Polish Charter should start a process leading to full identification with the Polish nation, with its culture, economic interests, civilizational aspirations and the desire to build the prosperity of the Polish state with all these consequences. Therefore, the Polish Charter should be part of the system of acquiring the best Polish human resources distributed around the world and part of a well-thought-out foreign policy on the integration of personnel necessary for the development of the Polish economy. It is therefore in the Polish national interest to conduct active socio-cadre policy on a global scale, as only building open to creativity of human resources will provide the Polish economy with innovative development impulses. The Polish Charter can become an instrument of recruitment for the R &amp; D sector, for industry, for the development of the economy following the famous American visa for H1B geniuses. The Free and Solidarnia Circle supports the proposed regulations, at the same time signaling the need to formulate a new strategy for Poles and Poles abroad beyond the current legislation. [[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 17 min. 55 hours 18 min 10]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of items 19, 20 and 21 of the agenda: 19. First reading of the bill presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the rules for repayment of certain debts resulting from credit and loan agreements (print No 811). 20. First reading of the parliamentary draft law on the restructuring of loans denominated or indexed to a currency other than the Polish currency and on the introduction of a ban on granting such loans (Print No 729). 21. First reading of the parliamentary draft law on special rules for the restructuring of foreign exchange housing loans in connection with the change of foreign exchange rates to the Polish currency (print No 877). I ask the Secretary of State at the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Maciej Łopiński, to present the justification for the bill contained in the print no. 811.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u417	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill, which I am presenting today on behalf of the President of the Republic, should be regarded as the first step taken by the State towards restoring equality between the parties to certain credit agreements. This equality has been affected in our assessment in the case of foreign currency credit agreements. The banks have used their dominant role in this type of credit relationship over the years, shaping it in such a way as to benefit on the one hand and free themselves from risk on the other. Irregularities in the provision and handling of foreign currency loans have been reported for many years. Unfortunately, the relevant state institutions were passive in their resolution and today we are in a situation where the scale of these irregularities threatens the financial system in Poland and thus threatens the economy to a large extent. In our view, this condition obliges the legislator to intervene, the main objective of which, in addition to restoring equality between the parties to credit agreements, will be to reduce systemic risk. Interference with existing legal relations is permitted for the reasons described in detail in the justification of the project. The opponents of this regulation are mainly raising the principle of non-retroactivity. Let us remember, however, that the prohibition of retroactivity is not an absolute prohibition. It should also be noted that the banking sector could and should expect regulation to restore the balance of the parties to the legal relationship in question, in some respects. It should be noted that there are already precedents in Polish law as regards the state's legal interference with legal relations that are ongoing or even expired in order to protect consumers. An example of the State's corrective action in the area of mortgages denominated or indexed to foreign currencies is the Act of 29 July 2011 amending the Banking Law Act and certain other laws, so-called. It's an anti-predition bill. Furthermore, the planned intervention cannot be identified, which some people are already trying to do today, with interference in the law of the third power in the State, i.e. in the laws of the courts. The adoption of the law in this area falls within the scope of Parliament's statutory powers. The courts in Poland do not create the law, but they do it, enjoying the quality of independence. The legislator and the executive authorities are required to shape the law underlying the action of the courts. The admissibility of legal intervention in this matter is probably irrefutable for Members of Parliament, since two further parliamentary legislative proposals on this issue will be discussed today, both involving an intervention and both providing for the action of the law backwards. The events of recent months also show that the courts increasingly take into account the position of borrowers that banks have unlawfully treated them in various aspects of the execution of credit agreements. This may in the future put banks in a difficult situation to satisfy courtly confirmed claims of a relatively large group of customers. The President of the Republic is still in favour of a comprehensive and complete solution for the borrower and a systemic solution to the foreign currency credit and loans crisis. A banquet. Therefore, the solution for foreign exchange spreads is the first stage of measures of a Sanitation nature. The president accepted the draft regulatory and supervisory activities proposed by the National Bank of Poland. Through the appropriate steps of the bodies in the Financial Stability Committee, i.e. Minister of Finance, Financial Supervision Commission, National Bank, Banking Guarantee Fund, inter alia by radically increasing the capital requirements of banks with the loans or loans described in their portfolios, it can be made impossible for banks to continue to maintain their currency-related loans. These measures are intended to give rise to concrete bank responses, i.e. above all, to refer to borrowers proposals for favourable changes to credit agreements, resulting in their transformation into gold credit agreements. The President of the National Bank of Poland, on the day of sending the project to the Marshal of the Sejm, stated that the process of conversion would be extended over time, but it would be a reasonable time, not too long, but too short. Distribution during this process will be an important element in maintaining financial stability. The office of the President of the Republic of Poland is now awaiting detailed proposals and will monitor their implementation on an ongoing basis. If the stimulated voluntary restructuring process does not produce visible results, the President of the Republic of Poland is determined and his firm is prepared to return to the concept of a legal solution to this crisis. However, a plan has been chosen which, following the recovery of undue benefits, retains financial security and reduces legal risks, allows effective termination of the case. Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! Aware of the differences in the assessment of the project presented and more broadly, the need for statutory intervention in the field of foreign currency loans, I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland is affected every day by statements of citizens describing their difficult life situation in connection with the operation of such loans. In order to understand to the end what problem we are dealing with, it is worth getting acquainted with such letters. President Andrzej Duda already during the presidential campaign, but later on he stressed the need to resolve the issue of foreign currency loans as a threat to the financial security of the state and with negative social consequences. In autumn 2015, the President's Office of the Republic of Poland intensified its work on the draft regulation. From October to December 2015, a series of meetings took place with representatives of the social side, notably the association of the bank's unlawfulness, who presented their expectations regarding the future law and the circumstances justifying its adoption. In view of the fact that some of the proposed solutions were difficult to accept in the context of compliance with the Constitution, the firm proposed its own concept of regulation on 15 January 2016. This concept was the subject of opinions formulated in the media as well as correspondence addressed to the firm. The potential financial impact of the implementation of this concept was presented in February 2016. National Bank of Poland on the margins of the annual report on the stability of the financial system. It was also assessed in terms of financial implications by the Financial Supervision Authority. In view of the wealth of legal and economic opinions submitted to the January proposal, the firm was appointed on 8 April. a special team to develop changes and additions to the proposal for a bill on how to restore equality between the parties of some credit agreements and loan agreements. The team completed its work at the end of June and some of its comments were taken into account in the bill submitted. It should be stressed that in parallel with the dialogue with the social side, the President's Office continued its discussions with representatives of the banking sector. During the project, a series of meetings took place with representatives of the Polish Bank Association, presidents of the boards of many banks operating in Poland, participating in the granting of loans denominated or indexed to foreign currencies. A group of bank managers also participated on equal terms with the social side in the debate organised by the Polish President's Office on 9 June. under the name "Responsible solution to foreign exchange loans'. This debate was a public summary of all actions to date. During this process, the arguments of all participants in the regulatory process were heard: public institutions, organisations, borrowers and the banking sector. The experts of the firm also met several times with representatives of leading audit firms operating in Poland, which are involved in the financial audit of Polish banks and companies from the financial sector on a daily basis. The subject of these meetings was the possibility of spreading over the balance sheet the effects of the various solutions proposed by the Polish President's Office. The project I am presenting today uses part of the work that has been developed during the above mentioned work. I assume that most of the recommendations submitted during them will translate into non-legislative actions undertaken by the Financial Stability Committee, the Ministry of Finance or the Financial Supervision Commission. High Izbo! Loans to foreign currency were granted in Poland mainly to persons who did not earn in foreign currency from 2000 to 2013 when the Financial Supervision Commission indicated in Recommendation S on good practices in the management of credit exposures secured by mortgage that, I quote: The Bank should only provide mortgage-backed loans to retail customers in the currency in which they obtain income, including for high-income customers. That's the end of the quote. Loans, despite their determination by foreign exchange loans, were very rare. Literally. It was not the payment of the borrower's currency, but merely the determination of the relationship between the amount of the zloty benefit and later the amount of the instalments and the currency. At the end of 2015, the number of loans granted to consumers to foreign currency amounted to more than 713 thousand in Poland. and amounted to over PLN 173 billion, of which to the Swiss franc was denominated or indexed 562 thousand. contracts worth PLN 144 billion. Most of them are mortgage-backed loans, among which the dominant group is residential loans. The question of the scope of clauses used in credit agreements denominated and indexed is debatable, which should be treated as abusive clauses, i.e. in the light of the law. In view of many court rulings and entries in the register of abusive clauses, these doubts have already been practically resolved with regard to clauses allowing banks to convert tranches and loan instalments using exchange tables introduced and set unilaterally by banks, i.e. so-called. spreads. In the Court's view, it is first of all up to the fact that the spread was, in practice, an additional loan commission established only in the context of their own decision and partly collected on the date of payment and partly on the payment of subsequent instalments. The removal of the effects of these clauses is the subject of the project I am presenting. What are the main principles of regulation, Members and Members? Since bank spreads have been established in a manner contrary to applicable law and the benefits collected for their application are undue benefits, creditors will be required to repay them. As far as the scope of the Law is concerned, the regulation concerns indexed and denominated credit agreements and indexed and denomination loans for which a mortgage on immovable property has been established. The purpose of the restriction in the form of a mortgage collateral criterion is to cover the scope of the credit regulation which was used to buy housing, house or parcel, i.e. to satisfy the consumer's housing needs. The draft law covers credit agreements concluded from 1 July 2000 to 26 August 2011, when the Anti-Predition Act allowing repayment of loans in foreign currency entered into force, which significantly reduced the cost for the client which had previously been due to the spread bank. As regards the subject matter, the regulation is addressed to consumers within the meaning of the Civil Code, but also to individuals engaged in a single business activity if they have not made depreciation write-offs on immovable property and did not include the cost of obtaining income from the business activity of interest and other charges under the credit agreement. The regulation provides for an obligation to reimburse benefits collected at the start-up and repayment of a credit for a spread higher than acceptable. The repayment may take the form of a reduction in the capital remaining to be repaid in the case of active loans or payments in the case of expired loans. We assume that the spread, if already collected, should be determined in a fair relation to the level of spreads that are collected in market practice. It was assumed that this fair spread should not exceed by more than 0,5% respectively, the rate of purchase or sale of a given currency announced by the National Bank of Poland. The amounts resulting from the use of higher spreads will be reimbursed. The project envisages a quota limit of 350 000. zł, which is the amount of the capital of the credit in respect of which the consumer is entitled to request a reduction in the capital or repayment of the overpayment. This limit may be exhausted in one credit or benefit from it in respect of more credits. In view of the main assumptions of the regulation, the need to reimburse unduly collected benefits resulting from the application of unauthorised contractual clauses, it would of course be appropriate to reimburse these benefits in full, regardless of the amount of credit granted. Introduction of a limit of 350 thousand in the project On the one hand, it is dictated by the need to balance the legitimate rights of consumers and, on the other hand, by the need to ensure the stability of the financial system. We assume that the legislator has the right to do so, while it is the right of the consumer to demand a refund of more money in legal proceedings. In addition, we must take into account that the legislator has the right to set certain limits on consumer protection, expressed at least in terms of typical benefits in terms of relations with consumers. This is the case in the 2011 Consumer Credit Act, setting the maximum amount of such credit at PLN 255 550. The proposed project adopted an amount of almost 100,000. zloty higher. Firstly, this is due to the fact that mortgage loans are, in principle, higher than consumer loans. Secondly, even if there are no specific limits to the value of a typical housing loan, over a period of several years in the foreign currency credit market, it is possible to determine the average value of the loan that was taken up at that time and to enable the person with minimal own contribution to the large Polish city to purchase the apartment. The designer has set this value for the amount of 350 000 already mentioned. zł. The proposal requires repayment of amounts resulting from the use of excessive spreads with interest of half the statutory interest. These interest rates are intended to compensate the consumer for the decrease in the purchasing power of money and the lost benefits. The proposed interest rate is close to the interest that the consumer could have obtained if he had placed his money in secure financial investments, e.g. in Treasury bonds. The regulation provides for the application procedure for the exercise of the powers provided for therein. The draft shall indicate a time limit of 6 months, calculated from the date of entry into force of the Act, for the consumer to submit a request for written information by the creditor on the use or non-use in the contract concluded with the consumer, upon payment, as well as the payment of the credit, of an undue currency spread. The creditor will be required to draw up and send the above information within 30 days of receipt of the consumer's application. The consumer's receipt of information on the application of an undue spread to him will open the possibility of applying for a reduction in the capital of the credit, which may take place within 12 months. The consumer whose contract has expired will also be entitled to submit an application and thus to receive the calculation and reimbursement of the excess payment. It does not matter whether the termination of the contract was caused by the creditor's termination or by the payment of the credit by the consumer. The exercise of the powers provided for by the law will not restrict the right of the consumer to initiate or continue judicial proceedings in respect of claims relating to a credit agreement. In the draft we propose an obligation of the Financial Supervision Commission to exercise control over the enforcement by creditors of the provisions of the Act. In the framework of this audit, the Financial Supervision Commission will be empowered to impose a penalty on the creditor for failing to comply with the obligations laid down by law. It is also envisaged to exempt income from the refund of the personal income tax spread. According to the draft, the regulation will enter into force 30 days after the date of publication. The costs of the entry into force of the Act depend on the scale of the exercise of the powers provided by the borrowers. With the scenario that claims for reimbursement of benefits will be submitted by all entitled persons, it is possible that they will amount to PLN 3.6 to 4 billion. High Izbo! I would like to ask for a positive assessment of the project submitted and for it to be adopted as proposed. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u423	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! We are discussing a very important social issue concerning the resolution of the loan problem that was granted between 2000 and 2011 - especially during this period - in which unfortunately illegible and hurting terms of contracts were offered to borrowers. All three projects that have emerged here are moving towards solving this burning problem, but they represent a completely different philosophy. While the presidential project submitted in this regard is about mitigating the negative effects of banks' collection of benefits by returning part of the benefits they have obtained from the use of foreign exchange spreads, the other two projects aim to convert the borrowings. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to make a few comments on these projects. First of all, I'm going to discuss the issue of the Presidential project in print No. 811. The aim of this project is to take measures to compensate for the effects of banks' collection of foreign exchange spreads in the granting of mortgage loans which were taken to buy real estate, most often housing, houses, parcels. The Act concerns mortgage or mortgage contracts, indexed or denominated to foreign currencies, most often to the Swiss franc, concluded between 1 July 2000 and 26 August 2011, when the amendment of the Banking Law of 29 July 2011, so-called. The anti-spread law has created the possibility for the borrower to purchase the currency on the market on its own. The problem is that when granting a loan in a foreign currency, the bank converted the amount of the loan into Polish currency at a rate less favourable to the borrower than would result from the rate of purchase of the foreign currency announced by the National Bank of Poland on the day on which the amount of the loan or its tranches was converted into the Polish currency. This applies to so-called. loans denominated, also in reverse situations, i.e. the granting of loans in Polish currency, which according to the contract were converted into foreign currency, I mean an indexed loan. Actual exchange rates were used lower than the sale rate of the foreign currency announced on a given day by the National Bank of Poland. Such banking practices were unilaterally imposed on borrowers. We know that, we know that. The President's representative, when submitting this proposal, outlined in detail the context in which these loans were drawn. The banks' practice consisted in the fact that the difference between the bank rate and the market rate constituted a bank spread and resulted in a capital increase for the benefit of banks and a loss for borrowers. The solutions did not allow consumers to verify the method of repayment, because contractual clauses did not determine the level of spreads applied by banks. Such behaviour of banks qualifies as a form of unfair market practices affecting the interests of the weaker party, consumers. It's not. the only situation in which banks apply stronger solutions. I mean, for example. loans so called an old wallet or, as who prefers, Balcerowiczowska granted in the 1990s, when both changes in interest rates without prior agreement were applied unilaterally, as well as other solutions which caused the Poles to borrow about 5 billion loans for housing purposes at the time, had already paid 7 billion, and added the state budget of 28 billion, i.e. 35 billion. Of course, these payments are still ongoing. These citizens cry and pay. The reduction in revenue by PLN 1.2 billion will therefore represent only 2% revenue of the entire sector. The project submitted by Kukiz15, in print No. 729, will have a positive impact and will generate significant socio-economic benefits. Firstly, it will contribute to the release of real estate, which will be able to be sold without problems, because with today's level of debt exceeding significantly the value of these real estates they are practically excluded from the market and at the same time their owners are tied to the place and cannot move, e.g. in search of a job, in another city. Secondly, the project will increase transactions on the market. More resources could be spent on consumption instead of unduly profiting banks. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u437	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position on draft laws, and I will no longer read their titles, from print No. 811, is a presidential and parliamentary project from print No. 729 and 877. We are launching a debate on a very difficult but at the same time a very important problem, and it is very important. Indexed and denominated loans to foreign currencies were granted in Poland to non-profit-making persons in foreign currency between 2000 and 2013. At the end of 2015, the number of loans granted to consumers indexed or denominated into foreign currency amounted to more than 713 thousand in Poland. and amounted to PLN 173 billion, of which PLN 562,000 respectively. - so many credit agreements, so many loans - and PLN 144 billion were loans related to the Swiss franc. Most of these loans are mortgaged and their dominant group are residential loans. The accumulation of foreign exchange loans took place between 2005 and 2008. In 2008, the value of foreign exchange loans was twice as high as the value of gold loans. These loans had significantly lower interest rates than residential loans in PLN. The first worrying events emerged with the economic crisis in 2008 and the increase in the franc rate. The climax took place in mid-January 2015 when the Swiss central bank decided to stop defending the Swiss franc exchange rate. During that period, it turned out that loans denominated or indexed were, despite regular repayment of the loan, often higher levels of debt than the initial amount borrowed. It is natural that borrowers feel deceived and trapped by banks. From a logical point of view, but also actually these loans were not paid or repaid in a foreign currency. The three aid projects submitted to the French people in general are an attempt to assist those who have borrowed loans denominated or indexed. Shortly, synthetically, I will try to characterize these solutions. The following aid to the franchise standardizes the bill drawn up by them themselves, submitted in the High House by the Kukiz Group15, print No 729. This project is the easiest solution and applies to loans denominated or indexed after 1 January 2000, provided that the period for which the loan was granted exceeds 60 months. Thus, the Act covers long-term loans which are generally mortgaged and relate to credit agreements which have been fully executed or terminated. It concerns not only consumers within the meaning of the Civil Code but also other natural persons who have concluded a credit agreement in connection with their business activities. The principle of restructuring is to convert a foreign currency loan into a gold loan using the interest rate so-called. the base rate, which is the reference rate of the National Bank of Poland plus the margin of the bank, i.e. not for free. The restructuring would have been carried out retroactively from the date on which the loan was granted. The Bank would fully settle the loan in the payment and repayment of its instalments. The project also prohibits the provision of loans whose settlement is based on a currency other than gold, i.e. the Polish currency. Draft Print Act No 877 submitted by Members of the Civic Platform provides for the conversion of a mortgage loan in foreign currency at the exchange rate of the date on which the restructuring agreement was drawn up and consists in the calculation of the difference between the value of the loan after the conversion and the amount of the debt calculated in the event that a contract was concluded in the past on the loan in Polish zlotys. The bank would have to pay off part of that amount, 50% , within a time limit depending on the amount of the difference calculated. In the first year, the bank would cancel out the most vulnerable in cases where the credit-to-security ratio exceeds 120%. In this way, the costs of redemption by the banks would be spread over time. The scheme could be used by persons whose credit value for collateral is higher than 80% and the property is their first real estate and fulfils the surface conditions, i.e. in the case of apartments up to 100 m2, and the house - up to 150 m2. In multi-child families, surface standards do not apply. The presidential project is to cover credit agreements concluded from 1 July 2000 until the entry into force of the Anti-Spread Act. According to the project, spreads would be returned at a level higher than acceptable. Spreads would be returned in the case of a loan granted up to a maximum of 350 000. PLN per person. They are also to be repaid when the credit agreement has expired, has been terminated or the loan has been repaid. The presidential project was negatively assessed by the Polish Bank Association, with doubts assessed by the National Bank of Poland and generally strongly criticised by the franchise themselves. Franchised borrowers in the number of 500 thousand. The president's position is considered treason. They therefore request that this project be eliminated from the legislative process. They stress that the presidential project is completely contrary to Mr Andrzej Duda's many-time electoral announcements, as well as his announcements as President of the Republic. The President himself and his officials, instead of apologizing to the voters for their irresponsibleness and making promises that cannot be fulfilled, have insisted and fought, telling the franchise that this is a solution good for them, for the borrowers. In the Communication of the President's Office of the Republic of Poland we read that long-term proposed solutions are beneficial for borrowers themselves, because they take into account the care of economic development. This is also a narration - it must be acknowledged as different from the President's promises - also continued Minister Andrzej Dera in Polsata News on 3 August 2016. He said then, I repeat his words: differently you look at the state, being a candidate for president, and otherwise, as you already are a president, seeing what the consequences are. It's bringing it in. So the president failed. Besides, I don't think it's the only time he failed, he said something else, and he did something else. It is also important to stress, High Izbo, that it is very clear that banks, institutions of public trust have failed in all this. This problem cannot be solved without consequences for banks, which have specialized in these harmful offers for borrowers. Fortunately, the problem does not concern the entire banking network in Poland. It only applies to five or several banks, let's say. We must not prolong the subject without an effect on solving this problem. Help must be given to fraudulent borrowers. With regard to the evaluation of the projects discussed today, the most far-reaching and most beneficial for fraudulent borrowers is the project contained in print No. 729, but the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party does not want to close this discussion and asks to debate all three of them in the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u543	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I will start with why there is no overall solution to the problem and the mechanism for the conversion of credits presented by me today by the High House. Well, the project, as I have already emphasised, is the first step in solving problems related to loans to foreign currencies. We have two problems right now. The first one is still felt by customers by banks that are significantly different from market currency spreads, and the second one is an increasing foreign exchange risk and increasing customer debt. The proposal presented today by the High House introduces statutory mechanisms to limit the effects of the first of these problems. The second problem, i.e. The elimination of exchange rate risk is a problem facing bodies operating within the Financial Stability Committee over the coming months, as announced at a conference with the participation of President Adam Glapiński. The bill is a compromise. Like any compromise solution, none of the parties fully satisfied. From the point of view of safeguarding the stability of the Polish banking system, it is not possible to solve all problems related to indexed or denominated loans on a one-off basis. I do not know if you remember, but at the beginning of January this year the President's Office presented a proposal to solve the problems in a comprehensive, three-point proposal, where the problem of spreads was only one point. The second point referred to the conversion of loans after the so-called "credits'. the fair exchange rate, which was related to the costs incurred by consumers receiving loans denominated or indexed to foreign currencies with gold credits after the so-called. It's a fair course. Finally, the third element of this project was the introduction of the debt relief rule for the transfer of real estate charged with this mortgage. What happened? Why did we decide in August this year to hand over only one point from this three-point programme to the High House? Well, a few things happened. The financial crisis on the Italian market has intensified, the financial crisis on the German market has intensified and we have finally had to deal with Brexit. All of this caused us to adopt the position of the National Bank of Poland, which suggested that the issues of conversion should be left to non-legislative action, leave as an action within the Financial Stability Committee of the National Bank of Poland, the Financial Supervision Commission and the Ministry of Finance, which will increase capital requirements, lead banks to be interested in voluntary transfer of these loans, which, of course, reduces the risk of challenging the law which the High Sejm would like to adopt in international arbitration. All this has led to the fact that the project from August this year is one point, only applies to spreads, but the firm will monitor the activities of financial supervision, the Ministry of Finance, the National Bank of Poland and if we consider that these actions do not produce the expected results, we are prepared to present to the High House such a comprehensive and global bill. The costs would be different for the financial sector - although I would rather talk about the unearned benefits rather than the costs - of tens of billions of zlotys and of 1 billion or 2 billion a year. In accordance with the interpretation of the Constitutional Tribunal, these provisions require the State to take such measures as strengthen the bond between family members and, in particular, the bond between parents and children and between spouses. In the view of the Constitutional Court, these provisions oblige the State authorities to take positive action to strengthen the ties between family members and not weaken them. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u585	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am very pleased to be able, on behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, to present a draft resolution to the Commission on the recognition of a draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the establishment of criteria and mechanisms for determining the Member State responsible for examining an application for international protection lodged in one of the Member States by a third-country national or a stateless person as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. The Commission carried out its first reading of the draft at its meeting on 5 October 2016 and adopted a report recommending the adoption of the draft by the High House. This proposal for a Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council with a signature COM (2016) 270 infringes the principle of subsidiarity as the proposed Regulation does not ensure that the objectives of the intended action at Union level are achieved better than those of national action. The stated objective of the draft regulation is to address the problem when a Member State faces a disproportionate number of applications for international protection for which it is responsible under the proposed regulation. This objective is to be achieved by introducing a mechanism for the redistribution of refugees between Member States, so-called. corrective allocation mechanism. This mechanism would allow for automatic activation of the instrument in emergency and crisis situations. This project will not achieve the above objective better than if the Member States were to act on the basis of national regulations. The important thing is that the solution is defined as the so-called solution. the reference key does not take into account the scale of Member States' commitment to operational support provided to other Member States, to have an external border between the European Union and the reception or integration capacity of the Member State concerned. However, all these elements are important for assessing the current and potential burden on Member States in the context of the migration crisis. High Izbo! It should be noted that, at the same time, the use of automation in the chapter of applicants for international protection between Member States will in fact reduce the level of management of migration waves in terms of quality. I will use a hypothetical example: if, as a result of this mechanism, Poland would be obliged to accept applicants who entered a Member State located far away from Poland from a neighbouring country vis-à-vis that Member State, and then in a short time, Poland itself would experience considerable migratory pressure from nationals of the neighbouring State, e.g. Ukraine, beyond the possibilities of the Polish asylum system, is asylum seekers from the neighbouring country with Poland as a result of this automated corrective allocation mechanism will be sent to other, far-reaching Member States. However, the reception of asylum seekers in a Member State neighbouring their country of origin is preferable, for example because of a better knowledge of the culture and realities of the neighbouring country, which facilitates the process of granting international protection and, in the longer term, integration activities. At the same time, a very important thing is the introduction of the so-called regulation. Financial solidarity and a commitment to the so-called "payments." the solidarity contribution in the event that a Member State temporarily suspends its participation in this corrective allocation mechanism should be understood as a financial penalty for those countries which refuse to accept migrants and an attempt to put pressure on the Member State reluctant to accept refugees. The amount of the so-called solidarity contribution of 250 000. The euro for each applicant for international protection paid to the Member State which is definitively recognised as responsible for examining the application in question should be assessed as particularly high. Moreover, due to the high costs resulting from the need for solidarity contributions, the proposed solution globally, on a scale of all Member States, is less effective than the asylum arrangements that individual Member States can currently apply. Consequently, it must be considered that the proposed solutions contained in the draft regulation infringe the principle of subsidiarity, and therefore, High Izbo, I ask once again for this draft resolution to be adopted. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

We all know how many areas Poland has benefited from this aid, only a blind man would not notice it. At the moment, he needs help, for example. Greece. How can we expect financial solidarity and not to cut funds for cohesion funds if we do not try to demonstrate our willingness to solidarity with the countries that need this assistance in solving the refugee problem? ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u593	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Modern Club on the recognition of the draft Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the establishment of criteria and mechanisms for determining the Member State responsible for examining an application for international protection lodged in one of the Member States by a third-country national or a stateless person as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity - for the time being the title of the resolution. I would like to refer first of all to the resolution, because it is neither time nor the public enough to address the issue of refugees. Ladies and gentlemen, the proposal proposed today does not address specific proposed amendments to existing legislation, but responds to the proposal for a directive as a whole. This is a logical error because no Member State is able to contribute to the harmonisation of the criteria for the processing of asylum applications across the European Union. The main allegation that the proposed directive is breaking the subsidiarity principle is also missed. The migration crisis has shown that the countries left alone are not doing well. Hence we need to work out a mechanism, but I would like to make it clear that as a modern club we also disagree with the idea that an automatic allocation mechanism or the amount of 250 000. The euro is not a good solution. We believe that other criteria should be taken into account when determining this. Another mistake in this resolution arises at the outset of the document, because the subsidiarity principle cannot be checked by national Parliaments in isolation from the other principles set out in the Treaties, such as the principle of proportionality and the principle of solidarity. As regards the principle of proportionality, it could, if any, be an argument for not adopting a fee rule of 250 000. the euro as compensation for refusing to grant international protection to an asylum seeker. It could also be argued that the fee is disproportionate to the financial capacity of certain countries. As regards the principle of solidarity, it is a fact that some countries, as is the case with Poland, constitute a border between the European Union and the Schengen area and, therefore, there may be an excessive burden on the number of asylum applications in some countries. As a result of participating in the agreement, it is in the interest of all signatories that the burden is even, the conditions for the reception of refugees are uniform and the rules are harmonious. What may be surprising, although the officials responsible for conducting the case on behalf of the Polish government in the Union constantly repeat the phrase about flexible solidarity, i.e. in their understanding of matching the ability to receive refugees to the level of economic development of the host country, vainly look in the draft resolution for references to the principle of solidarity. The arguments used in the draft resolution also mix the question of the real burden of the countries concerned with the hypothetical burden. And so a real burden on the influx of immigrants, and thus the number of asylum applications occur in Greece, Italy or Spain, and in the case of Poland these are hypothetical burdens, so far. Just check how many positive asylum applications have been issued in recent years on behalf of the Republic of Poland. There is and never has been an option for any country to consider an asylum application from neighbouring countries because of their cultural proximity or availability of translators, which is also included in the resolution. Asylum seekers do not choose, ladies and gentlemen, countries where they want to settle because they can adapt to it. Refugees flee from the dangers of life, health, the inability to survive themselves and their families. This is the result of the Convention on Human Rights and the Geneva Convention. However, ladies and gentlemen, as regards the main elements of the dispute contained in this directive, namely the automatic allocation mechanism and charges of 250 000. The euro solidarity contribution for the suspension in this mechanism for every unaccepted refugee, we agree with the resolution and we will vote in favour. However, I would like to stress that this resolution does not exempt the government from developing the best, common to the European Union as a whole, solutions to today's very difficult problems at EU level. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u595	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Free and Solidarnia Circle in relation to the draft resolution expressing the position of the Polish Sejm on the assessment of the compatibility of the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council establishing the criteria and mechanisms for determining the Member State responsible for examining an application for international protection lodged in one of the Member States by a third-country national or a stateless person with the principle of subsidiarity. Since the entry into force of the Treaty of Lisbon, as we read in the explanatory memorandum, national parliaments have contributed to the proper functioning of the European Union, ensure respect for the principle of subsidiarity, in accordance with the procedures laid down in the Protocol on the application of the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality annexed to the Treaty on European Union and the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in which the criteria for assessing subsidiarity are indicated. The Protocol also provides that any Chamber of the National Parliament may send a reasoned opinion to the Presidents of the European Parliament, the Council and the Commission stating the reasons why it considers that the draft does not comply with the principle of subsidiarity. In an opinion annexed to the proposed resolution, the applicant has demonstrated that the proposal for a Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council is not in conformity with the principle of subsidiarity for the reasons set out in the resolution. In the light of experience so far and the possible scale of future migration, the proposed solution potentially leads to financial consequences that cannot be borne by the Member State concerned. Moreover, due to the high costs resulting from the need for solidarity contributions, the proposed solution globally, on a scale of all Member States, is less effective than the asylum arrangements that individual Member States can apply. However, when we see the need for solidarity, we must bear in mind the mistakes that the European Union makes when trying to eliminate the problem of Muslim migrants. It is our duty to point out bad rules that do not solve the issue of migrants, but only to shift responsibility for third country errors to other countries. The Circle of Free and Solidarni is in favour of the proposed resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
