ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u599	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the bill on the works concession or service contract. I would like to point out that the bill under review is the result of the work of the subcommittee, which I had the honour of chairing. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development then gave a positive opinion and approved the draft law. On 5 September 2016, the Sejm adopted the above law virtually unanimously, i.e. by 431 votes in favour, 1 against, 0 abstentions. During the further legislative process of the Senate of the Republic of Poland on 6 October 2016, 42 amendments were introduced by the following formula: 12 drafting amendments, 11 clarification amendments, five amendments introducing similar solutions, four amendments relating to the standardisation of all notices, four amendments relating to the implementation of European Union law on EU directives and six major amendments, including the approval of the conclusion of a concession contract according to the so-called. In-house rules, that's a non-negotiable procedure. High Izbo! Allow me to present the skeleton and the objectives of the bill. Well, as the name says, it is a law on the award of works concessions or services. This is an important act because it concerns a very large stream of public money within the framework of a public-private partnership. This law is a specific legislative form allowing investment to be carried out without its own financial contribution. In the case of traditional public contracts, the contractor, after passing the tendering procedure, carries out the task and receives remuneration from the contracting authority. In this case, no longer the contractor, and the concessionaire carries out the task and does not receive remuneration directly from the Contracting Authority, but obtains the right to operate the building or provide the services covered by the concession. Thus, the concessionaire assumes the economic risk of the project and must ensure that it is in a position to make an adequate profit by compensating for the financial resources put in place for the investment and, if necessary, making a profit. Therefore, this law provides for simplified procedures for the selection of a concessionaire. The Contracting Authority will be able to arrange the procedure for the selection of the concessionaire, as it considers convenient, while maintaining the rules of conduct, i.e. the principles of fair competition, equal treatment of economic operators, proportionality and transparency. High Izbo! The basic change proposed by the Senate of the Republic of Poland is to allow the conclusion of the concession according to the so-called. In-house rules, i.e. the conclusion of a concession contract without a tendering procedure with the audited entity, e.g. a municipal company. So this is a very important change that gives a great deal of confidence and competence to the contracting authority vis-à-vis the concessionaire. I assume that, for various reasons, this form of investment in the future will be a very popular form, because it offers an opportunity to develop without having to invest its own funds. In view of the above, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the amendments proposed by the Senate and to accept the proposed bill as a whole. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u600	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Waldemar Olejniczak will be the first to speak. You're welcome.

What else? This conference with the historians we invited took place outside the walls of the Sejm. The question is, did it have to happen that these historians had to walk around Warsaw, we had to rent a hall for this meeting, after all it is a puriazum. These things can't happen in our parliament, because that's why we fought for freedom, that's why we had the fall of a commune, that's why we have the conquerors of democracy to discuss here, to be able to exchange thoughts here. Therefore, Mr Marshal, I appeal to the Bureau, and I appeal to the secretariat that this should never happen again in the future. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u628	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! For 2 years, EU sanctions have been in place against Russia due to its involvement in the conflict in Ukraine. On the one hand, these sanctions have proved ineffective, on the other hand, they cause retaliatory steps on the part of Russia and contribute to significant losses of the Polish economy, in particular Polish agriculture. For 2 years Polish agricultural products, Polish meat, apples and other products have not been allowed into Russia as a result of administrative decisions of the Russian sanitary authorities due to the alleged significant content of pesticides, antibiotics, hormones or other harmful substances. Meanwhile, annual imports of coal into Poland are increasing, reaching 8 million tons per year. More than half of the imported coal comes from Russia. Despite Russian difficulties for Polish exporters, Russian exports of coal to Poland are very good. I am not enthusiastic about continuing to maintain trade sanctions against Russia. In principle, I am in favour of free trade benefiting all its participants. However, I would like Poland to be a serious country fighting hard for its interests. I would like the Polish authorities, instead of begging in Brussels for a little productive irritation of our eastern neighbour, to be able to give themselves a hard and customs response to the hostile actions of the Russian side. If Polish meat is harmful to Russian consumers, why is Russian coal not yet harmful to the Polish environment? If Polish apples contain too many pesticides, why doesn't Russian coal contain too much sulphur? Why can't the Polish authorities decide on their own hard and concrete response to Russian sanctions? I hope that the answers to these questions will soon be answered. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u630	Message statement on the 70th anniversary of Antony Zubryd's death. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! October 24th is the 70th. The anniversary of the death of Anthony Zubrid ps. Attaboy - Petty Officer of the Polish Army, Major of the National Armed Forces, commander of the guerrilla unit of the National Armed Forces. Antoni Zubrid was born in Sanok, at the end of World War I, on September 4, 1918. In his hometown he graduated from Men's School No. King Władysław Jagiełła, and in 1936 he graduated from Petty Officers' School of Infantry for Junior No. 2 in Śrem. He was then platooned and assigned to 40. Lviv Children's Infantry Regiment. After the outbreak of World War II during the September campaign, he fought for Warsaw and was promoted to the rank of sergeant and awarded the Cross of Valiant. After the fights, he entered German captivity, but immediately after his release, he returned to Sanoka and began his conspiracy. After being assigned to work in Germany, escape from transport and several weeks of hiding, he returned to the city. After he was captured trying to cross the German-Soviet border in 1940, he cooperated with Soviet intelligence. During his diagnosis he met his future wife, Janina Prazyńska. In 1941, he was arrested by the Gestapo and sentenced to death. Janina, as Anthony's wife, got a life sentence. The Zubrid managed to escape when he was being led to an execution. He was then associated with the National Army. After the Red Army entered Sanocchini, he volunteered to serve in the Sanok UB. His obscure past and differences in the assessment of fitness led him to be under special surveillance. As a result, after exposing Zhubrid's double activity, in June 1945 it was decided to release him from the Ministry. But before that, he freed some of the arrested and went underground. Żubryd managed to subdue the surrounding Poak guerrilla groups quite quickly and create a more than 100-person group, which took the name of the Independent Operational Battalion of the National Armed Forces. The fame of the battalion and its commander quickly spread the whole country. He was successful in dealing with Stalin's camera. Battalion was mainly involved in ambushing UB officers, MO, municipal offices, railway stations, trains, shops. There have also been sabotage, robbery and requisition of food. He managed to lead to the death of Chief of Staff 8. Infantry Division Colonel. Theodora Rajewski, as well as the head of the Faculty of Politics and Education 8. Infantry Division, Captain. Abraham Preminger. In the liquidation of the SBO NSZ took part 4. Rzeszow Land CWB Brigade. Zhubryda's unit was eventually destroyed by military information personnel from 8. The Infantry Division, who brought their own people into the squad. When the situation became hopeless, Antoni and his wife planned to get to Austria. Unfortunately, his companion, Jerzy Vaulin, had been recruited by UB several months earlier as a secret associate. He killed Antony Zubryd with a shot to the back of the head. A moment later, he shot Janina, who was eight months pregnant. The remains of Anthony and Janina Zubrid were not found. During the Polish People's Republic Antoni Zubryd was called a bandit and thief. It was not until 28 June 1994 that the Provincial Court in Rzeszów rehabilitated Antoni Żubryd, recognizing him as a hero fighting for the independent existence of the Polish state. Thank you.

Poland needs knowledge, experience and wisdom of seniors, so any stimulation of older people's activities through science and culture, health promotion actions, access to modern information technologies and the popularisation of solidarity between generations is highly indicated and extremely necessary. October abounds with numerous opening ceremonies for the new academic year. As a parliamentarians, I also have the honour to take part in numerous inaugurations of the academic year in the universities of the third century. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-20-sejm-28-2.u633	Declaration on respect for the rights and values forming the foundations of Polish families in connection with the work on the resolution on the establishment of Family Rights Day. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today the Sejm of the Republic of Poland took part in the plenary session in the work on the resolution on the establishment of Family Rights Day. Among these many roads the family is the first and for many reasons the most important. It is a universal road, remaining every time a special road, the only and unique one, just as every human being is unique. The family is the way he can't separate himself from. After all, normally every one of us in the family comes into the world, so one can say that the family owes itself to being human. And if there is no family in this coming into the world and entering the world of man, it is always a breach, and it is not extremely disturbing and painful, which then bears on the whole life - so St. John Paul II in his letter "Gratissimam sane" in 1994. Today, it is still important to stress that the family has always been and will be the highest value. For all of us, it is an area of love, security, posture and identity, a pillar of individual functioning and the proper shape of society. The family must be protected, especially now that it is threatened by various negative trends, what brings fashion to all liberalism. The family is also a kind of carrier of the future - it plays a key role - it creates a new life, but it also provides care for the elderly. It is therefore a perfect place to learn respect, care, responsibility and solidarity of generations. It is the first community we belong to. Unfortunately, we also all see negative demographic trends that affect the functioning of families - clearly highlighted in our voivodship. They also need to be accompanied by a painful problem for many families of economic emigration. In the face of these challenges, the family cannot be left to itself - it should be provided with the care of the state, and the role of the rulers is to support it. Now it's really the first time in this dimension that's happened because of the 500+ family program. It will certainly affect the resolution of many everyday problems, but it is also an expression of recognition for the role that the family plays in society, and an emphasis on how much the future of Poland depends on its proper functioning. During his speech in March 2001 during the 8th Gniezno Congress, President Lech Kaczyński said: If we have a mission in a united Europe - and we have, I believe, many - the mission to show the values of the family is particularly important. I think that Poland, and above all Poles, will find the strength to meet this mission. I wish you to do this so that our families are filled with love, mutual respect, that they build attitudes of empathy towards another man, that they be a safe harbor from which you can sail into the wide waters of the modern world, but to which you can always return, knowing that someone is waiting for us.
